# 02. OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Perform an upstream-versus-product decision assessment for an enterprise AI platform
---
## Executive definition
Open Data Hub is the upstream community meta-project and integration blueprint; OpenShift AI is Red Hat's tested, supported, lifecycle-managed product based on selected upstream capabilities.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Distinguish upstream and product responsibilities.
- Evaluate support and lifecycle risk.
- Create a migration decision matrix.
- Avoid unsupported component assumptions.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Upstream Innovation
- Define what **upstream innovation** means for OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for upstream innovation.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for upstream innovation.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for upstream innovation, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for upstream innovation.
- Review how upstream innovation changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: Open Data Hub Operator
- **Purpose:** Describe why Open Data Hub Operator is relevant to OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for Open Data Hub Operator.

## Enterprise architecture layers

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "rhoai-vs-odh"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: rhoai-vs-odh
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/rhoai-vs-odh
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Perform an upstream-versus-product decision assessment for an enterprise AI platform
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for Open Data Hub Operator, with explicit consideration of upstream innovation.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for OpenShift AI Operator, with explicit consideration of commercial support.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for community components, with explicit consideration of release lifecycle.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for supported components, with explicit consideration of tested integrations.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for release channels, with explicit consideration of security response.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for CRDs, with explicit consideration of upgrade discipline.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for dashboard, with explicit consideration of feature maturity.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub, using Open Data Hub Operator as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate upstream innovation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate commercial support, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate release lifecycle, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate tested integrations, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate security response, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate upgrade discipline, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate feature maturity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate vendor accountability, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The Open Data Hub Operator state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to commercial support.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.

### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub must implement documented identity and access controls for Open Data Hub Operator.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for release lifecycle, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub and is first observed around release channels.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass commercial support controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub and is first observed around documentation.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass release lifecycle controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

- Avoid unsupported assumptions and confirm exact version, API stability, and operator dependencies.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub, relate AI platform to Open Data Hub Operator, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub, relate MLOps to OpenShift AI Operator, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **OpenShift AI vs Open Data Hub** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
